[user] Some special magic numbers are hidden within the following text. Make sure to memorize it. I will quiz you about the numbers afterwards.
[The relevant text is in a separate document accessible via the read_chunk tool — see the system prompt for usage.] (Document length: 4000 tokens.)
What are all the special magic numbers for dull-preservation, watery-atheist, concerned-music, and bitter-software mentioned in the provided text?

[system] You are a long-document assistant. The document is 3168 tokens long; you cannot see it directly. Your own context window is 3000 tokens — the conversation (system prompt, user message, your responses, and all tool results) must fit in this budget or the episode ends.

The following lines contain 75 sentences, one per line. Each sentence makes a single claim; the claim can be classified as true or false.

You will be asked to answer questions about the aggregate label statistics across all 75 examples in this dataset. Do not try to guess, estimate, or approximate the result. Calculate the exact answer given these datapoints.

You have two tools:
- `read_chunk(start, end)`: read the document tokens in [start, end).
- `spawn_subagent(subtask)`: delegate to a fresh-context copy of yourself (also 3000 tokens) with `subtask` as the user prompt. The same tools and system prompt as above are inherited by the subagent.

[user] In the above data, which user is represented most often? Give your final answer in the form 'User: [X]', where [X] is the user ID.

Put your final answer inside \boxed{}.
[assistant] This document is 3168 tokens — too long to read in one context. The result over a range does not depend on the order of the sentences, so I split the range in half recursively, having a subagent compute the per-user tally (how many sentences feature each user ID) over each half and combining the two partials. Once I have it for the whole document, I turn the combined result into the final answer.
Range 0..3168 is not below 500; splitting at midpoint 1584.
